<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed integer <i>r</i> ≥ 4, every <i>K</i><sub><i>r</i></sub>-free graph on <i>n</i> vertices with average degree <i>d</i> ≥ 2 has an independent set of size at least $`c_r n\log d/d`$, where $`c_r\gt 0`$ depends only on <i>r</i>. This proves the fixed-clique-size independence conjecture of Ajtai, Erdős, Komlós and Szemerédi.

- **c-stmt-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $r\ge4$, there is a constant $c_r>0$ such that every finite simple $K_r$-free graph $G$ with $n$ vertices and average degree $d\ge2$ satisfies $$\alpha(G)\ge c_r\frac{n\log d}{d}.$$
  - Explanation: Proves a uniform logarithmic improvement in the independence bound for graphs excluding a fixed clique.
- **c-stmt-lem-optimizer** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-optimizer)): For every nonempty finite simple graph $G$, the function $F_G$ attains its maximum. Every maximizer $w$ has positive coordinates and satisfies $$\label{eq:stationarity} \log(1/w_v)=w(N_G(v)) \qquad(v\in V(G)).$$ In particular, $w_v\le1$. For every nonnegative vector $q$, $$\label{eq:optimizer-divergence} 0\le F_G(w)-F_G(q) =\sum_v\left(q_v\log\frac{q_v}{w_v}-q_v+w_v\right) +M_G(q-w).$$ Here a term with $q_v=0$ in the sum equals $w_v$.
  - Explanation: Establishes positive maximizing weights, their stationarity equations and the exact variational identity.
- **c-stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers)): Let $r\ge2$ be an integer, let $G$ be a finite $K_r$-free graph with positive vertex weights $w$, and put $s=w(V(G))$. For every $0<\varepsilon\le1/2$ there are jointly distributed random variables $(m_v)_{v\in V(G)}$ such that $$\label{eq:sparse-multipliers} 0\le m_v\le b_r(\varepsilon) :=2^{r(r-2)}\varepsilon^{-(r-2)},\qquad \mathbb E m_v=1,\qquad \mathbb E M_G((w_vm_v)_v)\le\varepsilon s^2.$$ Their joint distribution can be taken to have finite support.
  - Explanation: Constructs a finite joint law preserving each weight in expectation while reducing expected edge mass.
- **c-stmt-lem-cross-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cross-mass)): Let $r\ge2$ be an integer and let $w$ maximize $F_G$ on a nonempty finite $K_r$-free graph $G$. For every $x\ge1$ and all vertex sets $S,D$ with $w(S),w(D)\le e^x$, $$\label{eq:optimizer-cross-mass} e_w(S,D)\le C_r g_x(w(S)), \qquad C_r=16r^2.$$ The sets $S$ and $D$ need not be disjoint.
  - Explanation: Bounds directed cross mass for maximizing weights, including overlapping vertex sets.
- **c-stmt-thm-weighted-triangles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-weighted-triangles)): For every $C\ge1$ there is a constant $C_{\triangle}(C)$ with the following property. If $G$ is a finite simple graph with positive vertex weights $w$ satisfying <a href="#eq:cross-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cross-mass">[eq:cross-mass]</a>, then $$T_G(w)\le C_{\triangle}(C)M_G(w).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds triangle mass by edge mass using only the cross-mass hypothesis.
- **c-stmt-lem-invariant-preservation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-invariant-preservation)): Let $(G,w)$ satisfy <a href="#eq:cross-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cross-mass">[eq:cross-mass]</a> with constant $C$. Let $H$ be a finite simple graph and let $\pi:V(H)\to V(G)$ satisfy the following conditions:* 1. *every edge $\{a,b\}$ of $H$ maps to the edge $\{\pi(a),\pi(b)\}$ of $G$;* 2. *the map $(a,b)\mapsto(\pi(a),\pi(b))$ is injective on directed edges of $H$.* *Give each $a\in V(H)$ the weight $\widetilde w_a=w_{\pi(a)}$. Then $(H,\widetilde w)$ satisfies <a href="#eq:cross-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cross-mass">[eq:cross-mass]</a> with the same constant $C$. Moreover, $$\begin{gathered} M_H(\widetilde w)\le M_G(w),\qquad T_H(\widetilde w)\le T_G(w),\\ \widetilde w(N_H(a))\le w(N_G(\pi(a))) \quad\text{for every }a\in V(H). \end{gathered}$$ These conclusions are preserved under any finite composition of such maps.
  - Explanation: Shows that weight-preserving graph maps injective on directed edges preserve the cross-mass condition and control masses.
- **c-stmt-lem-triangle-peeling** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-triangle-peeling)): For a positive-weighted finite simple graph $G$, put $$s_u=w(N_G(u)),\qquad c_{uv}=w(N_G(u)\cap N_G(v)),\qquad h_u=\sum_{v\in N_G(u)}w_vc_{uv}.$$ Thus $h_u=2M_{G[N_G(u)]}(w)$ is twice the edge mass inside the neighborhood of $u$. Then $$\label{eq:triangle-normalizations} \sum_{\{u,v\}\in E(G)}w_uw_vc_{uv}=3T_G(w), \qquad \sum_{u\in V(G)}w_uh_u=6T_G(w).$$ In particular, if every neighborhood has weight at most $L$, then $$\label{eq:terminal-triangle-bound} T_G(w)\le \frac{L}{3}M_G(w).$$* *For every $\tau>0$, there is a spanning subgraph $G'$ obtained by successive edge deletions such that every edge of $G'$ has common-neighbor weight at least $\tau$ and $$\label{eq:peeling-loss} T_{G'}(w)\ge T_G(w)-\tau M_G(w).$$ For each nonisolated vertex of $G'$, its neighborhood and common-neighbor weights, computed in $G'$, satisfy $$s_u\ge\tau,\qquad h_u\ge\tau s_u.$$ If $G$ satisfies <a href="#eq:cross-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cross-mass">[eq:cross-mass]</a>, then so does $G'$, with the same constant.
  - Explanation: Counts weighted triangles and deletes edges with small common-neighbor weight at controlled triangle loss.
- **c-stmt-lem-entropy-smoothing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-smoothing)): Under <a href="#eq:cross-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cross-mass">[eq:cross-mass]</a> and <a href="#eq:walk-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:walk-hypotheses">[eq:walk-hypotheses]</a>, put $$E_C=42C^2+2C,\qquad A_C=10+E_C,\qquad B_C=2\sqrt{A_C+1}, \qquad R=\lceil x\rceil.$$ Each $K_u^j$ is reversible with stationary measure $\pi_u$, and $$K_u^j(v,y)\le xw_y\qquad(j\ge0). \label{eq:walk-density}$$ Moreover, $$-\log x\le H_0\le H_1\le\cdots\le H_R\le A_C\log x. \label{eq:entropy-upper}$$ There exists $j\in\{0,\ldots,R-1\}$ such that, under the ordered triangle law, $$\mathbb E\bigl\\|K_u^j(v,\cdot)-K_u^j(z,\cdot)\bigr\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le B_C\sqrt{\frac{\log x}{x}}. \label{eq:triangle-row-distance}$$
  - Explanation: Controls averaged neighborhood-walk entropy and finds a common time with close rows under the ordered triangle law.
- **c-stmt-lem-shared-rejection** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-shared-rejection)): Let $I$ and $J$ be finite nonempty sets, and let $(p_i)_{i\in I}$ be probability distributions on $J$. There are jointly distributed random variables $(Y_i)_{i\in I}$ such that $Y_i$ has law $p_i$ and, for every $i,i'\in I$, $$\mathbb P(Y_i\ne Y_{i'}) \le \frac{2\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}} {1+\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}} \le 2\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.$$ The resulting joint law has finite support.
  - Explanation: Couples finite probability distributions with controlled pairwise disagreement.
- **c-stmt-lem-reverse-admissibility** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-reverse-admissibility)): The probability that $Y_{u,v}$ is inadmissible, averaged over $(u,v)\sim\mu$, is at most $$\label{eq:inadmissibility-bound} \delta_x:=\frac{(A_C+1)\log x}{\sqrt x+\log x}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds inadmissibility averaged under the directed-edge law, rather than separately for each row.
- **c-stmt-lem-weighted-splitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-splitting)): There is a choice of these labels for which the projection $(u,y)\mapsto u$ preserves weights, maps directed edges injectively to directed edges of $H$, and satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \max_{a\in V(H')}w(N_{H'}(a))&\le e^{\sqrt x}, \label{eq:split-neighborhood}\\ T_{H'}(w)&\ge(1-a_C\log x/\sqrt x)T_H(w), \label{eq:split-triangles} \end{aligned}$$ where $a_C=6(B_C+A_C+1)$.
  - Explanation: Selects labels that reduce neighborhood weights while retaining triangle mass and directed-edge injectivity.
- **c-stmt-prop-neighborhood-reduction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-neighborhood-reduction)): Let $G$ be a finite positive-vertex-weighted graph satisfying the cross-mass condition <a href="#eq:cross-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cross-mass">[eq:cross-mass]</a> with constant $C\ge1$, and suppose that $\max_vw(N_G(v))\le e^x$ for some $x>X(C)$. There is a finite positive-vertex-weighted graph $G'$ with a weight-preserving map to $G$ that is injective on directed edges, such that $$\begin{aligned} \max_vw(N_{G'}(v))&\le e^{\sqrt x},\label{eq:step-neighborhood}\\ T_{G'}(w)&\ge \left(1-a_C\frac{\log x}{\sqrt x}\right)T_G(w) -\frac{M_G(w)}x.\label{eq:step-triangles} \end{aligned}$$ It also satisfies the same cross-mass condition, and $M_{G'}(w)\le M_G(w)$.
  - Explanation: Combines peeling and splitting into a neighborhood reduction preserving the cross-mass condition.
- **c-stmt-prop-maximum-degree** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-maximum-degree)): For every integer $r\ge4$, every real number $\Delta\ge3$, and every finite $K_r$-free graph $G$ with $n$ vertices and maximum degree at most $\Delta$, $$\alpha(G)\ge \frac{F_G^*}{D_r\log\Delta} \qquad\text{and}\qquad \alpha(G)\ge \frac{n\log\Delta}{4D_r\Delta}.$$
  - Explanation: Derives independent-set bounds for a fixed maximum-degree parameter from the variational optimum.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r\\ge2$ be an integer, let $G$ be a finite $K_r$-free graph with positive vertex weights $w$, and put $s=w(V(G))$.         "source_id": "PM-184.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-invariant-preservation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(G,w)$ satisfy <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a> with constant $C$. Let $H$ be a finite simple graph and let $\\pi:V(H)\\to V(G)$ satisfy the following conditions:*\n\n1.  *every edge $\\{a,b\\}$ of $H$ maps to the edge $\\{\\pi(a),\\pi(b)\\}$ of $G$;*\n\n2.  *the map $(a,b)\\mapsto(\\pi(a),\\pi(b))$ is injective on directed edges of $H$.*\n\n*Give each $a\\in V(H)$ the weight $\\widetilde w_a=w_{\\pi(a)}$. Then $(H,\\widetilde w)$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a> with the same constant $C$. Moreover, $$\\begin{gathered}\n M_H(\\widetilde w)\\le M_G(w),\\qquad\n T_H(\\widetilde w)\\le T_G(w),\\\\\n \\widetilde w(N_H(a))\\le w(N_G(\\pi(a)))\n \\quad\\text{for every }a\\in V(H).\n\\end{gathered}$$ These conclusions are preserved under any finite composition of such maps.",
        "source_id": "PM-184.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-invariant-preservation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(G,w)$ satisfy <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a> with constant $C$. Let $H$ be a finite simple graph and let $\\pi:V(H)\\to V(G)$ satisfy the following conditions:*\n\n1.  *every edge $\\{a,b\\}$ of $H$ maps to the edge $\\{\\pi(a),\\pi(b)\\}$ of $G$;*\n\n2.  *the map $(a,b)\\mapsto(\\pi(a),\\pi(b))$ is injective on directed edges of $H$.*\n\n*Give each $a\\in V(H)$ the weight $\\widetilde w_a=w_{\\pi(a)}$. Then $(H,\\widetilde w)$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a> with the same constant $C$. Moreover, $$\\begin{gathered}\n M_H(\\widetilde w)\\le M_G(w),\\qquad\n T_H(\\widetilde w)\\le T_G(w),\\\\\n \\widetilde w(N_H(a))\\le w(N_G(\\pi(a)))\n \\quad\\text{for every }a\\in V(H).\n\\end{gathered}$$ These conclusions are preserved under any finite composition of such maps.",
          "source_id": "PM-184.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(G,w)$ satisfy <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a> with constant $C$. Then $(H,\\widetilde w)$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a> with the same constant $C$. Moreover, $$\\begin{gathered}\n M_H(\\widetilde w)\\le M_G(w),\\qquad\n T_H(\\widetilde w)\\le T_G(w),\\\\\n \\widetilde w(N_H(a))\\le w(N_G(\\pi(a)))\n \\quad\\text{for every }a\\in V(H).\n\\end{gathered}$$ These conclusions are preserved under any finite composition of such maps.",
      "explanation": "Shows that weight-preserving graph maps injective on directed edges preserve the cross-mass condition and control masses.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-triangle-peeling",
        "exact_quote": "For a positive-weighted finite simple graph $G$, put $$s_u=w(N_G(u)),\\qquad\n c_{uv}=w(N_G(u)\\cap N_G(v)),\\qquad\n h_u=\\sum_{v\\in N_G(u)}w_vc_{uv}.$$ Thus $h_u=2M_{G[N_G(u)]}(w)$ is twice the edge mass inside the neighborhood of $u$. Then $$\\label{eq:triangle-normalizations}\n \\sum_{\\{u,v\\}\\in E(G)}w_uw_vc_{uv}=3T_G(w),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{u\\in V(G)}w_uh_u=6T_G(w).$$ In particular, if every neighborhood has weight at most $L$, then $$\\label{eq:terminal-triangle-bound}\n T_G(w)\\le \\frac{L}{3}M_G(w).$$*\n\n*For every $\\tau>0$, there is a spanning subgraph $G'$ obtained by successive edge deletions such that every edge of $G'$ has common-neighbor weight at least $\\tau$ and $$\\label{eq:peeling-loss}\n T_{G'}(w)\\ge T_G(w)-\\tau M_G(w).$$ For each nonisolated vertex of $G'$, its neighborhood and common-neighbor weights, computed in $G'$, satisfy $$s_u\\ge\\tau,\\qquad h_u\\ge\\tau s_u.$$ If $G$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a>, then so does $G'$, with the same constant.",
        "source_id": "PM-184.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-triangle-peeling",
          "exact_quote": "For a positive-weighted finite simple graph $G$, put $$s_u=w(N_G(u)),\\qquad\n c_{uv}=w(N_G(u)\\cap N_G(v)),\\qquad\n h_u=\\sum_{v\\in N_G(u)}w_vc_{uv}.$$ Thus $h_u=2M_{G[N_G(u)]}(w)$ is twice the edge mass inside the neighborhood of $u$. Then $$\\label{eq:triangle-normalizations}\n \\sum_{\\{u,v\\}\\in E(G)}w_uw_vc_{uv}=3T_G(w),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{u\\in V(G)}w_uh_u=6T_G(w).$$ In particular, if every neighborhood has weight at most $L$, then $$\\label{eq:terminal-triangle-bound}\n T_G(w)\\le \\frac{L}{3}M_G(w).$$*\n\n*For every $\\tau>0$, there is a spanning subgraph $G'$ obtained by successive edge deletions such that every edge of $G'$ has common-neighbor weight at least $\\tau$ and $$\\label{eq:peeling-loss}\n T_{G'}(w)\\ge T_G(w)-\\tau M_G(w).$$ For each nonisolated vertex of $G'$, its neighborhood and common-neighbor weights, computed in $G'$, satisfy $$s_u\\ge\\tau,\\qquad h_u\\ge\\tau s_u.$$ If $G$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a>, then so does $G'$, with the same constant.",
          "source_id": "PM-184.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For a positive-weighted finite simple graph $G$, put $$s_u=w(N_G(u)),\\qquad\n c_{uv}=w(N_G(u)\\cap N_G(v)),\\qquad\n h_u=\\sum_{v\\in N_G(u)}w_vc_{uv}.$$ Thus $h_u=2M_{G[N_G(u)]}(w)$ is twice the edge mass inside the neighborhood of $u$. Then $$\\label{eq:triangle-normalizations}\n \\sum_{\\{u,v\\}\\in E(G)}w_uw_vc_{uv}=3T_G(w),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{u\\in V(G)}w_uh_u=6T_G(w).$$ In particular, if every neighborhood has weight at most $L$, then $$\\label{eq:terminal-triangle-bound}\n T_G(w)\\le \\frac{L}{3}M_G(w).$$*\n\n*For every $\\tau>0$, there is a spanning subgraph $G'$ obtained by successive edge deletions such that every edge of $G'$ has common-neighbor weight at least $\\tau$ and $$\\label{eq:peeling-loss}\n T_{G'}(w)\\ge T_G(w)-\\tau M_G(w).$$ For each nonisolated vertex of $G'$, its neighborhood and common-neighbor weights, computed in $G'$, satisfy $$s_u\\ge\\tau,\\qquad h_u\\ge\\tau s_u.$$ If $G$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a>, then so does $G'$, with the same constant.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:walk-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:walk-hypotheses\">[eq:walk-hypotheses]</a>, put $$E_C=42C^2+2C,\\qquad A_C=10+E_C,\\qquad B_C=2\\sqrt{A_C+1},\n \\qquad R=\\lceil x\\rceil.$$ Each $K_u^j$ is reversible with stationary measure $\\pi_u$, and $$K_u^j(v,y)\\le xw_y\\qquad(j\\ge0).\n \\label{eq:walk-density}$$ Moreover, $$-\\log x\\le H_0\\le H_1\\le\\cdots\\le H_R\\le A_C\\log x.\n \\label{eq:entropy-upper}$$ There exists $j\\in\\{0,\\ldots,R-1\\}$ such that, under the ordered triangle law, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl\\|K_u^j(v,\\cdot)-K_u^j(z,\\cdot)\\bigr\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\le B_C\\sqrt{\\frac{\\log x}{x}}.\n \\label{eq:triangle-row-distance}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-184.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-smoothing",
          "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:walk-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:walk-hypotheses\">[eq:walk-hypotheses]</a>, put $$E_C=42C^2+2C,\\qquad A_C=10+E_C,\\qquad B_C=2\\sqrt{A_C+1},\n \\qquad R=\\lceil x\\rceil.$$ Each $K_u^j$ is reversible with stationary measure $\\pi_u$, and $$K_u^j(v,y)\\le xw_y\\qquad(j\\ge0).\n \\label{eq:walk-density}$$ Moreover, $$-\\log x\\le H_0\\le H_1\\le\\cdots\\le H_R\\le A_C\\log x.\n \\label{eq:entropy-upper}$$ There exists $j\\in\\{0,\\ldots,R-1\\}$ such that, under the ordered triangle law, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl\\|K_u^j(v,\\cdot)-K_u^j(z,\\cdot)\\bigr\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\le B_C\\sqrt{\\frac{\\log x}{x}}.\n \\label{eq:triangle-row-distance}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-184.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under <a href=\"#eq:cross-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cross-mass\">[eq:cross-mass]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:walk-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:walk-hypotheses\">[eq:walk-hypotheses]</a>, put $$E_C=42C^2+2C,\\qquad A_C=10+E_C,\\qquad B_C=2\\sqrt{A_C+1},\n \\qquad R=\\lceil x\\rceil.$$ Each $K_u^j$ is reversible with stationary measure $\\pi_u$, and $$K_u^j(v,y)\\le xw_y\\qquad(j\\ge0).\n \\label{eq:walk-density}$$ Moreover, $$-\\log x\\le H_0\\le H_1\\le\\cdots\\le H_R\\le A_C\\log x.\n \\label{eq:entropy-upper}$$ There exists $j\\in\\{0,\\ldots,R-1\\}$ such that, under the ordered triangle law, $$\\mathbb E\\bigl\\|K_u^j(v,\\cdot)-K_u^j(z,\\cdot)\\bigr\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n \\le B_C\\sqrt{\\frac{\\log x}{x}}.\n \\label{eq:triangle-row-distance}$$",
      "explanation": "Controls averaged neighborhood-walk entropy and finds a common time with close rows under the ordered triangle law.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-shared-rejection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I$ and $J$ be finite nonempty sets, and let $(p_i)_{i\\in I}$ be probability distributions on $J$. There are jointly distributed random variables $(Y_i)_{i\\in I}$ such that $Y_i$ has law $p_i$ and, for every $i,i'\\in I$, $$\\mathbb P(Y_i\\ne Y_{i'})\n \\le \\frac{2\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n {1+\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n \\le 2\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}.$$ The resulting joint law has finite support.",
        "source_id": "PM-184.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-shared-rejection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $I$ and $J$ be finite nonempty sets, and let $(p_i)_{i\\in I}$ be probability distributions on $J$. There are jointly distributed random variables $(Y_i)_{i\\in I}$ such that $Y_i$ has law $p_i$ and, for every $i,i'\\in I$, $$\\mathbb P(Y_i\\ne Y_{i'})\n \\le \\frac{2\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n {1+\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n \\le 2\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}.$$ The resulting joint law has finite support.",
          "source_id": "PM-184.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $I$ and $J$ be finite nonempty sets, and let $(p_i)_{i\\in I}$ be probability distributions on $J$. There are jointly distributed random variables $(Y_i)_{i\\in I}$ such that $Y_i$ has law $p_i$ and, for every $i,i'\\in I$, $$\\mathbb P(Y_i\\ne Y_{i'})\n \\le \\frac{2\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n {1+\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n \\le 2\\|p_i-p_{i'}\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}.$$ The resulting joint law has finite support.",
      "explanation": "Couples finite probability distributions with controlled pairwise disagreement.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Independent set** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An independent set in a graph is a set of pairwise nonadjacent vertices.
- **Independence number** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a finite simple graph $G$, write $\alpha(G)$ for the largest size of an independent set.
- **Average degree** ([source](#sec:introduction)): If $G$ has $n>0$ vertices, its average degree is $d(G)=2\|E(G)\|/n$.
- **Clique exclusion** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The graph is $K_r$-free if no $r$ vertices are pairwise adjacent; throughout, exclusion means exclusion as an ordinary subgraph.
- **Edge and triangle masses** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For such a vector $w$, write $w(S)=\sum_{v\in S}w_v$, and define its edge and triangle masses by $$M_G(w)=\sum_{\{u,v\}\in E(G)}w_uw_v, \qquad T_G(w)=\sum_{\{u,v,z\}\text{ a triangle}}w_uw_vw_z.$$
- **Variational potential and optimum** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Define $$F_G(w)=\sum_{v\in V(G)}w_v(1-\log w_v)-M_G(w), \qquad F_G^*=\max_{w\ge0}F_G(w).$$
- **Cross-mass control function** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For $x\ge1$, define $$g_x(s)= \begin{cases} s\bigl(x+\log^+(1/s)\bigr),&s>0,\\ 0,&s=0, \end{cases} \qquad \log^+ a=\max\{0,\log a\}.$$
- **Cross-mass bound** ([source](#eq:cross-mass)): We say that $(G,w)$ satisfies the cross-mass bound with constant $C$ if, for every $x\ge1$ and every $A,B\subseteq V(G)$, $$\label{eq:cross-mass} w(A),w(B)\le e^x \quad\Longrightarrow\quad e_{G,w}(A,B)\le Cg_x(w(A)).$$
- **Local walk kernels** ([source](#sec:entropy)): On the finite set $N(u)$ define stochastic matrices $$P_u(v,z)=\frac{w_z\mathbf1_{\{vz\in E(G)\}}}{c_{uv}},\qquad Q_u=\frac{I+P_u}{2},\qquad K_u^j=P_uQ_u^j\quad(j\ge0). \label{eq:walk-kernels}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-main | \alpha(G) | ≥ | $c_r\frac{n\log d}{d}$ | The constant depends only on the fixed excluded clique size. |
| q-multiplier | b_r(\varepsilon) | = | $2^{r(r-2)}\varepsilon^{-(r-2)}$ | Integer clique size and positive precision within the stated interval. |
| q-cross-constant | C_r | = | $16r^2$ | The constant depends only on the excluded clique size. |
| q-cross | e_w(S,D) | ≤ | $C_r g_x(w(S))$ | Both set weights satisfy the displayed upper bound. |
| q-triangle | T_G(w) | ≤ | $C_{\triangle}(C)M_G(w)$ | The constant depends only on the cross-mass constant. |
| q-terminal | T_G(w) | ≤ | $\frac{L}{3}M_G(w)$ | Every neighborhood has weight at most the specified bound. |
| q-peeling | T_{G'}(w) | ≥ | $T_G(w)-\tau M_G(w)$ | Positive peeling threshold; common-neighbor weights are recomputed after each deletion. |
| q-density | K_u^j(v,y) | ≤ | $xw_y$ | All nonnegative walk indices under the cross-mass and walk hypotheses. |
| q-inadmissibility | \delta_x | = | $\frac{(A_C+1)\log x}{\sqrt x+\log x}$ | Average under the directed-edge law with the entropy and density bounds. |
| q-split-loss | a_C | = | $6(B_C+A_C+1)$ | Depends only on the cross-mass constant. |
| q-triangle-constant | C_\triangle(C) | = | $2(1+e^{X(C)}/3)$ | Threshold chosen by the displayed iteration inequality. |
| q-deletion-constant | D_r | = | $3+\frac32 B_r$ | Depends only on the excluded clique size. |
| q-max-degree | \alpha(G) | ≥ | $\frac{n\log\Delta}{4D_r\Delta}$ | The maximum-degree parameter stays fixed throughout the induction. |
| q-leading | c_r | = | $1/(16D_r)$ | Depends only on the fixed excluded clique size. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-stmt-lem-optimizer** [claim](#stmt-lem-optimizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-optimizer): Establishes positive maximizing weights, their stationarity equations and the exact variational identity. -> Establishes positive maximizing weights, their stationarity equations and the exact variational identity.
- **s-stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers** [claim](#stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sparse-multipliers): Constructs a finite joint law preserving each weight in expectation while reducing expected edge mass. -> Constructs a finite joint law preserving each weight in expectation while reducing expected edge mass.
- **s-stmt-lem-cross-mass** [claim](#stmt-lem-cross-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cross-mass): Bounds directed cross mass for maximizing weights, including overlapping vertex sets. -> Bounds directed cross mass for maximizing weights, including overlapping vertex sets.
- **s-stmt-lem-invariant-preservation** [claim](#stmt-lem-invariant-preservation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-invariant-preservation): Shows that weight-preserving graph maps injective on directed edges preserve the cross-mass condition and control masses. -> Shows that weight-preserving graph maps injective on directed edges preserve the cross-mass condition and control masses.
- **s-stmt-lem-triangle-peeling** [claim](#stmt-lem-triangle-peeling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-triangle-peeling): Counts weighted triangles and deletes edges with small common-neighbor weight at controlled triangle loss. -> Counts weighted triangles and deletes edges with small common-neighbor weight at controlled triangle loss.
- **s-stmt-lem-entropy-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-smoothing): Controls averaged neighborhood-walk entropy and finds a common time with close rows under the ordered triangle law. -> Controls averaged neighborhood-walk entropy and finds a common time with close rows under the ordered triangle law.
- **s-stmt-lem-shared-rejection** [claim](#stmt-lem-shared-rejection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shared-rejection): Couples finite probability distributions with controlled pairwise disagreement. -> Couples finite probability distributions with controlled pairwise disagreement.
- **s-stmt-lem-reverse-admissibility** [claim](#stmt-lem-reverse-admissibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reverse-admissibility): Bounds inadmissibility averaged under the directed-edge law, rather than separately for each row. -> Bounds inadmissibility averaged under the directed-edge law, rather than separately for each row.
- **s-stmt-lem-weighted-splitting** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-splitting): Selects labels that reduce neighborhood weights while retaining triangle mass and directed-edge injectivity. -> Selects labels that reduce neighborhood weights while retaining triangle mass and directed-edge injectivity.
- **s-stmt-prop-neighborhood-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-neighborhood-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-neighborhood-reduction): Combines peeling and splitting into a neighborhood reduction preserving the cross-mass condition. -> Combines peeling and splitting into a neighborhood reduction preserving the cross-mass condition.
- **s-stmt-thm-weighted-triangles** [claim](#stmt-thm-weighted-triangles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-neighborhood-reduction-2): Bounds triangle mass by edge mass using only the cross-mass hypothesis. -> Bounds triangle mass by edge mass using only the cross-mass hypothesis.
- **s-stmt-prop-maximum-degree** [claim](#stmt-prop-maximum-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-maximum-degree): Derives independent-set bounds for a fixed maximum-degree parameter from the variational optimum. -> Derives independent-set bounds for a fixed maximum-degree parameter from the variational optimum.
- **s-stmt-thm-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-maximum-degree-2): Proves a uniform logarithmic improvement in the independence bound for graphs excluding a fixed clique. -> Proves a uniform logarithmic improvement in the independence bound for graphs excluding a fixed clique.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-peeling**: Successive deletion of edges with common-neighbor weight below the threshold retains triangle mass above the threshold-dependent lower bound and leaves every edge with common-neighbor weight at least the threshold.
- **I-injection**: Weight-preserving vertex splitting preserves the cross-mass bound when directed edges map injectively to original directed edges.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-efficiency**: Treat the independence bound as an efficient independent-set algorithm. -> An existence proof supplies no efficiency guarantee.
- **A-duplicate**: Duplicate original edges while splitting vertices and inherit the cross-mass guarantee. -> Repeated preimages invalidate the directed-edge injectivity hypothesis.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The edge-peeling proof is a finite constructive procedure for positive-weighted finite simple graphs. This implementation covers rational weights and checks the peeling conclusion; it does not construct the independent set promised by the main theorem.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of finite simple graphs, positive rational weights and threshold.
# PRECONDITIONS: At most 6 vertices, distinct ordered undirected edges, positive weights and threshold.
# OUTPUTS: Remaining edges, deletion trace, original edge mass and retained triangle mass.
# TIME: O(n^3+n*m^2+m) exact rational operations per graph.
# SPACE: O(n+m) cells per graph, excluding batched input and emitted output.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic with unbounded integer numerators and denominators.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; no approximate output is returned.
import json,sys,itertools
from flint import fmpq

def peel(g):
    n=g['n']; w=list(map(fmpq,g['w'])); tau=fmpq(g['tau'])
    assert isinstance(n,int) and 0<=n<=6 and len(w)==n and all(t>0 for t in w) and tau>0
    edges=[tuple(e) for e in g['edges']]
    assert len(set(edges))==len(edges) and all(0<=a<b<n for a,b in edges)
    E=set(edges); original=set(E); trace=[]
    def common(a,b):
        return sum((w[z] for z in range(n) if tuple(sorted((a,z))) in E and tuple(sorted((b,z))) in E),fmpq(0))
    def triangle_mass():
        return sum((w[a]*w[b]*w[c] for a,b,c in itertools.combinations(range(n),3) if {(a,b),(a,c),(b,c)}<=E),fmpq(0))
    mass=sum((w[a]*w[b] for a,b in E),fmpq(0))
    for step in range(len(original)+1):
        doomed=next(((a,b) for a,b in sorted(E) if common(a,b)<tau),None)
        if doomed is None: break
        trace.append(list(doomed)); E.remove(doomed)
    return {'edges':list(map(list,sorted(E))),'trace':trace,'M':str(mass),'T':str(triangle_mass())}
print(json.dumps([peel(g) for g in json.loads(sys.stdin.read())],sort_keys=True))
```

    "output_contract": "A spanning subgraph, a legal deletion trace, original edge mass and retained triangle mass, with the terminal common-neighbor threshold and the source peeling-loss bound.",
    "reason": "The edge-peeling proof is a finite constructive procedure for positive-weighted finite simple graphs. This implementation covers rational weights and checks the peeling conclusion; it does not construct the independent set promised by the main theorem.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of finite simple graphs, positive rational weights and threshold.\n# PRECONDITIONS: At most 6 vertices, distinct ordered undirected edges, positive weights and threshold.\n# OUTPUTS: Remaining edges, deletion trace, original edge mass and retained triangle mass.\n# TIME: O(n^3+n*m^2+m) exact rational operations per graph.\n# SPACE: O(n+m) cells per graph, excluding batched input and emitted output.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic with unbounded integer numerators and denominators.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; no approximate output is returned.\nimport json,sys,itertools\nfrom flint import fmpq\n\ndef peel(g):\n    n=g['n']; w=list(map(fmpq,g['w'])); tau=fmpq(g['tau'])\n    assert isinstance(n,int) and 0<=n<=6 and len(w)==n and all(t>0 for t in w) and tau>0\n    edges=[tuple(e) for e in g['edges']]\n    assert len(set(edges))==len(edges) and all(0<=a<b<n for a,b in edges)\n    E=set(edges); original=set(E); trace=[]\n    def common(a,b):\n        return sum((w[z] for z in range(n) if tuple(sorted((a,z))) in E and tuple(sorted((b,z))) in E),fmpq(0))\n    def triangle_mass():\n        return sum((w[a]*w[b]*w[c] for a,b,c in itertools.combinations(range(n),3) if {(a,b),(a,c),(b,c)}<=E),fmpq(0))\n    mass=sum((w[a]*w[b] for a,b in E),fmpq(0))\n    for step in range(len(original)+1):\n        doomed=next(((a,b) for a,b in sorted(E) if common(a,b)<tau),None)\n        if doomed is None: break\n        trace.append(list(doomed)); E.remove(doomed)\n    return {'edges':list(map(list,sorted(E))),'trace':trace,'M':str(mass),'T':str(triangle_mass())}\nprint(json.dumps([peel(g) for g in json.loads(sys.stdin.read())],sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source establishes an independent-set existence bound. A software implementation contract is absent. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source treats finite simple graphs and independent sets. A game model or simulation contract is absent. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source gives a mathematical existence proof. A tool interface or executable extraction procedure is absent. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | stated | Successive deletion of edges whose current common-neighbor weight is below $\tau$ is a finite constructive subroutine. It controls triangle-mass loss and preserves the cross-mass condition when that condition already holds. This does not supply an efficient independent-set algorithm. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof uses graphs and vertex weights as mathematical objects. A data representation with operation guarantees is absent. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source gives graph-theoretic inequalities. An accelerator architecture or hardware execution contract is absent. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source gives an independence bound under clique exclusion. A cryptographic primitive or security contract is absent. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The entropy functional serves an extremal graph proof. A learning model or statistical guarantee is absent. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | For an independent-set objective on a graph satisfying the stated hypotheses, the theorem supplies an existential lower bound on the optimum. This is an inferred mathematical use; an optimisation algorithm is absent. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source couples probability distributions and splits graph vertices within a mathematical proof. A distributed protocol contract is absent. |
| other (Extremal graph theory) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The theorem directly establishes a uniform independence bound for clique-free finite simple graphs. This is a graph-theoretic result with no efficient algorithm claim. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-main-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The main independence bound requires a finite simple $K_r$-free graph, an integer $r\ge4$, and average degree $d\ge2$. Blocked use: ['Applying the main bound to graphs with loops or multiple edges, infinite graphs, unestablished clique exclusion, or parameters outside the stated regime.']
- **limit-ordinary-exclusion** (hypothesis): Clique exclusion is ordinary subgraph exclusion. Blocked use: ['Replacing ordinary clique exclusion by an unsupported local sparsity condition.']
- **limit-logarithm** (hypothesis): The logarithm convention is natural. Blocked use: ['Changing the logarithm convention while retaining the same coefficient.']
- **limit-constant** (approximation): The coefficient $c_r$ depends only on $r$ and is not optimized. Blocked use: ['Claiming an optimal leading constant or a practical optimized coefficient.']
- **limit-main-algorithm** (nonconstructive): The proof gives an existence result and makes no claim of an efficient independent-set algorithm. Blocked use: ['Treating the independence bound as an efficient extraction procedure or a runtime guarantee.']
- **limit-weighted-scope** (hypothesis): The weighted triangle theorem requires positive vertex weights and the cross-mass hypothesis; it does not require clique exclusion. Blocked use: ['Applying the triangle bound to arbitrary weights without proving the cross-mass condition.']
- **limit-splitting** (hypothesis): Splitting preserves the invariant when copies inherit their original weights and each original edge is retained at most once. Blocked use: ['Duplicating original edges across copies and claiming the preservation conclusion.']
- **limit-entropy-average** (counterexample_scope): The entropy and row-distance estimates control averages over the specified triangle distribution. Blocked use: ['Claiming a uniform bound for every individual row or every individual triangle.']
- **limit-empty-optimizer** (edge_case): The empty graph on no vertices has a separate optimum convention and no positive-coordinate assertion. Blocked use: ['Applying the nonempty optimizer positivity assertion to the zero-vertex case.']
- **limit-peeling** (hypothesis): Triangle peeling recomputes common-neighbor weights in the current graph before each deletion and terminates on a finite graph. Blocked use: ['Using stale original common-neighbor weights as a certificate that the final graph meets the threshold.']
- **limit-finite-law** (nonconstructive): Finite support of the shared coupling does not give a deterministic bound on the number of rejection trials. Blocked use: ['Interpreting almost-sure termination of shared rejection sampling as a fixed trial bound.']
- **limit-fixed-degree** (hypothesis): The maximum-degree induction keeps the same number $\Delta$ throughout deletion. Blocked use: ['Substituting a changing degree parameter into the stated potential-cost induction without another argument.']
- **limit-companions** (formal_scope): The companion Ramsey estimates are comparison results and are not inputs to this proof. Blocked use: ['Making the weighted triangle theorem depend on the companion Ramsey estimates.']

>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result gives a logarithmic improvement in the independence number of clique-free graphs. For every fixed integer $r\ge4$, there is $c_r>0$ such that every finite $K_r$-free simple graph on $n$ vertices with average degree $d\ge2$ has an independent set of size at least $c_r n\log d/d$. The constant depends only on $r$.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `averageDegree` | def |
| `logarithmic_independence_bound` | theorem |

Comparator source `CliqueFreeLog.lean` (577 bytes, sha256 `f2da8879eb520c1fcc7812d3d476f528b54dd7405c00a8f4c0488e212379c2d2`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace CliqueFreeLog

noncomputable def averageDegree {V : Type*} [Fintype V] (G : SimpleGraph V) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact 2 * (G.edgeFinset.card : ℝ) / (Fintype.card V : ℝ)

theorem logarithmic_independence_bound (r : ℕ) (hr : 4 ≤ r) :
    ∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧
      ∀ {V : Type} [Fintype V] (G : SimpleGraph V),
        G.CliqueFree r → 2 ≤ averageDegree G →
        c * (Fintype.card V : ℝ) * Real.log (averageDegree G) / averageDegree G ≤
          (G.indepNum : ℝ) := by
  sorry

end CliqueFreeLog

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every fixed integer <i>r</i> ≥ 4, every <i>K</i><sub><i>r</i></sub>-free graph on <i>n</i> vertices with average degree <i>d</i> ≥ 2 has an independent set of size at least $`c_r n\log d/d`$, where $`c_r\gt 0`$ depends only on <i>r</i>. This proves the fixed-clique-size independence conjecture of Ajtai, Erdős, Komlós and Szemerédi.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
